"The young lady said: 'Buras, put me down—I'm tired of being on your back.' The noble prince then took her hand, and they walked together, the young lady under a [shade tree], outside the northern slope."

### 9.
"'Brother, I'm hungry—find me some rice and fried chicken!' Buras hurried off into the village. 'Buras, yes, my lord!'"

### 10.
"Entering the village, he met an elder named Buyut Sudarga. Buras said: 'I beg your pardon, sir, may I ask for some rice and chicken?'"

### 11.
"Kyai Buyut asked: 'Who is it that sent you?' Buras replied softly: 'The one who sent me is my master, named Prince Jayengsari, who brings his younger sister along.'"

### 12.
"'The one asking for rice is still young, and both master and mistress are children of Susuhunan Prapen of Giri, who fled when Giri fell.'"

### 13.
"'And they have an older brother named Prince Jayengresmi, who also fled at the same time—he is the one they seek, but they have not yet found him.'"

### 14.
"'They have not even heard any news of him, which is why they have wandered as far as here.' Hearing this, Ki Buyut spoke gently to Buras: 'Boy, these are truly noble persons!'"

### 15.
"'Their birth is noble, sweet as honey, of royal lineage. I would guess they are of a hundred [noble] rank. If I may invite them to stop at my home—'"

### 16.
"Ki Buras replied with great pleasure: 'If it is not too much trouble for you, sir, I wish to present myself before the noble one.' Buras quickly went ahead and arrived before the young prince."

### 17.
"Bowing low, Kyai Buyut knelt in homage, sitting cross-legged with folded hands, and spoke softly: 'I offer my blessings and welcome—your arrival in this hamlet is a great fortune.'"

### 18.
"'It is as if flooded with honey, as if showered by a mountain of sugar, as if visited by a great god, with celestial maidens and divinities descending to my humble dwelling.'"

### 19.
"Such was the welcome of Ki Buyut. 'Uncle, I return your blessings and good will, confirming the joy in my heart. I am most grateful—we are equals, after all.'"

### 20.
"'May I ask, what is your name, sir?' Ki Buyut bowed and replied softly: 'The one you ask about is called Buyut Sudarga, appointed elder of this entire hamlet.'"

### 21.
"'In Tasari and all the surrounding area, I am trusted and relied upon. Please allow me, noble sir, together with your sister the princess, to rest in my home.'"

### 22.
"'I will devote myself wholly to preparing the house and providing food—let your eating and sleeping be my responsibility.'"

### 23.
"Jayengsari spoke sweetly: 'Yes, uncle, that will be fine.' He then accompanied Ki Buyut into the house, where Nyai Buyut bustled about."

### 24.
"She welcomed the young lady, carrying her at once: 'Oh, my lord, it is as if a heavenly light has fallen—your arrival here fills me with such gratitude!'"

### 25.
"Arriving at the house, they all sat on mats. The grandchildren stood in front, serving refreshments in the mountain manner. Ki Buyut spoke humbly:"

### 26.
"'Forgive my presumption, but I must offer this—it is but a modest remedy for hardship, such as it is. This is all the mountain provides. Please accept it, young hero.'"

### 27.
"'Indeed, uncle, do not be anxious—my heart is as pleased as if I had been accustomed here for a year.' Ki Buyut was exceedingly happy. Nyai Buyut spoke softly:"

### 28.
"'Princess, whatever you wish...' 'Yes, auntie, but first—take me to bathe.' 'Certainly, let us go to the lake.' The jewel was then carried."

### 29.
"After bathing, the young lady returned to sit with her brother, not far apart. They ate heartily of the offered food. Buras ate until he was stuffed, his belly distended."

### 30.
"The sun had fully set; it was the time of the full moon. The moon peeked over the mountain peak. Jayengsari was startled to see:"

### 31.
"To the south of the peak, glowing and emitting smoke. He asked: 'Uncle, what is that I see with the smoke?' Ki Buyut replied softly: 'That is Mount Brahma, the one glowing.'"

### 32.
"'Day and night it constantly emits smoke. Uncle, if possible tomorrow morning, I wish to see the peak of Mount Tengger.' Ki Buyut gladly agreed."

### 33.
"The night passes unspoken; morning comes. The prince said to his younger sister: 'Little sister, don't come along—I am going to the mountain peak. The road is very difficult.'"

### 34.
"'Just a little while, then I'll return. Buras, you stay here—play with your mistress.' The young lady Rancangkapti said: 'Brother, I'm not going! I want to play "market" with the mountain children.'"

### 35.
"She picked vegetables—seledri and greens—and gathered flowers of many colors. All day she did not want to return, playing in the garden with the little children crowding around."

### 36.
"Meanwhile the prince was on his way. Ki Buyut walked ahead, climbing toward the mountain peak, leaving behind the village of Tasari. Arriving at the peak, he looked out and saw:"

## Pangkur 1.

### 1.
"Below the southern peak lay a crater, wide and gaping. In the middle was a mountain like an overturned rice-steamer. By day the smoke was not visible. Ki Buyut said to the prince: 'That is its name.'"

### 2.
"'Tasari's Mount Brahma—some call it the Sea of Sand. The hill in the middle is Mount Brahma. The ground is all sand, covered with ash issuing from the Brahma crater.'"

### 3.
"'When the wind blows, the whiteness is dazzling to see. Because of the drifting sand, it is barren, without vegetation; moss and grass cannot take root. Thus it lies white and bare, gleaming under the sun's rays.'"

### 4.
"'The heat waves that rise above the sand form shimmering mirages like ocean waves. One might think it a sea, but it is not water—it appears as an ocean situated atop the mountain.'"

### 5.
"'What you say is true, uncle—can it be approached?' Ki Sudarga replied: 'It is best to descend.' They went down, the way extremely difficult, the path steep, overgrown, and rough, until they reached the expanse of sand."

### 6.
"The prince said further: 'Come, let us look at the peak of Mount Brahma and its crater.' Ki Buyut agreed and climbed, making his way to the summit. At the edge of the crater he stood, circling its rim."

### 7.
"Leaning over to look into the crater, he saw it was extremely deep, with many great stones visible. Besides the main crater there were many side vents, all emitting smoke, smelling of burning sulfur. The prince said: 'Let us go.'"

### 8.
"They descended to the base, breathing with difficulty from the mountain fumes. Both went down together. When they reached the flat ground, on the eastern side was a low hill, exceedingly beautiful to behold. The prince asked softly:"

### 9.
"'Uncle, what is that hill I see, so very lovely?' Ki Sudarga bowed and replied: 'That is the hermitage of Ngadisari, where dwells one called Ajar Satmaka, the spiritual leader of Mount Tengger.'"

### 10.
"'His many female dancers are exquisite, of many kinds, never disappointing. This is his nature: he still follows Buddha, with Lord Brahma as his guide. He is a great man of Prabalingga, who desires no praise.'"

### 11.
"The prince, hearing this, said: 'Uncle, if it is agreeable, tomorrow morning I will go to pay homage to Ki Ajar, to learn about his practices and the Buddhist-Brahma religion.' Ki Buyut agreed."

### 12.
"From the base they ascended, the return journey not difficult but lengthy. At the time of Asar they arrived back at Tasari. They met Rancangkapti, who was quickly lifted up, while asking: 'You weren't crying, were you?'"

### 13.
"'I left you for a whole day.' Niken Rancangkapti replied softly: 'Brother, the whole day I never came home—I was playing market in the back garden with many village girls, so absorbed I forgot to eat.'"

### 14.
"The prince was delighted to hear this. Arriving home, refreshments were already prepared. Nyai Buyut busily said: 'I have prepared the meal, offering it to your sister who was gone all day. I invited her but she refused, waiting for you.'"

### 15.
"The prince then ate with his sister, both enjoying the meal. When they had finished, Ki Buyut and Buras also ate heartily. After all were full, the剩下的 were cleared away. Ki Buyut remained in attendance."

### 16.
"Niken said to her brother: 'Brother, what did you see at the fiery mountain? Tell me about it—I'll listen while I sleep beside you.' The prince then told the story, and Niken Rancangkapti lay down to sleep."

### 17.
"The night passes unspoken. At dawn, those who had slept awoke. Nyai Buyut and Ki Buyut served the morning refreshments: warm drinks, food, corn porridge, hard wajik, jepen, canthèl, and cariping linjik."

### 18.
"Cassava cariping, kenthang, peanuts, sweet gembili and uwi, aromatic temulawak drink, young coconut with palm sugar. Kyai and Nyai Sudarga bowed and said: 'Please accept these offerings of food, my lord.'"

### 19.
"'Morning food—yes, uncle. Come now, little sister—what do you wish?' After the morning meal, Jayengsari said to Ki Buyut: 'Uncle, please accompany me to Ngadisari.'"

### 20.
"'To pay homage to Ki Ajar Satmaka.' 'Certainly, my child, I will follow. Nyai, you come too, with provisions—drinks and food for the road, in case the young lady wants refreshment, as it is rather far.'"

### 21.
"Everything was ready and they departed. The journey goes undescribed—they arrived at the clearing before the mountain. Ki Sudarga went first; all waited outside the village. Now Ajar Satmaka already knew that guests were coming."

### 22.
"'The prince and princess of noble lineage—prepare yourselves to receive them in the manner of religious observance.' When Ki Buyut arrived, he was immediately called before the hermit, bowing and reporting from beginning to end the purpose of their visit."

### 23.
"Kyai Ajar smiled and said: 'Ah, Buyut, bring them here at once. The knight who wishes to meet me—I will greet him outside the gate.' Ki Buyut withdrew and met Prince Jayengsari."

### 24.
"'Please, Your Highness, enter.' The sage did not wait inside but came out before the gate, leaving his meditation place. Rancangkapti was led by her brother in front. They bowed to the ascetic, and both were embraced."

### 25.
"They were brought into the hermitage and sat together with the sage. He summoned his female dancers quickly—those already adorned in fine attire—who came forward, their movements graceful and refined, their demeanor in gambuh style, seeking to create a reverent atmosphere."

### 26.
"The sage commanded the dancers: 'Serve the refreshments quickly.' The dancers served at once, presenting various foods—all the mountain's provisions were laid before them. The sage was truly skilled in entertaining guests."

### 27.
"While they ate with pleasure, Kyai Buyut Sudarga took his leave, returning to Tasari village with his family. Permission was granted; he bowed and withdrew. Prince Jayengsari remained at Ngadisari."

### 28.
"He stayed with the ascetic, never parting day or night. His younger sister, the jewel-like princess, was happy being cared for by the dancers. Thus they remained long at Ngadisari. Now on one occasion, the prince spoke, bowing in homage:"

### 29.
"'O my lord, what are the practices and customs of the exalted Buddhist religion that you continually observe?' The sage replied softly: 'Ah, my child, know this—the religion that I practice.'"

### 30.
"'The people of all Mount Tengger truly follow Lord Brahma. Once every year, on an auspicious day, men and women, old and young, bring offerings of various foods and clothing, ascending Mount Brahma.'"

### 31.
"'When they reach the peak, their offerings are quickly cast into the crater, accompanied by prayers of praise to Lord Brahma who dwells in the fire. Unmarried youths and maidens, when they go to work,'"

### 32.
"'In the fields, gardens, and plantations, each carries a jungkat [betel-box] as they please, made of wood or buffalo horn. If a young man wishes to marry a maiden, or a maiden wishes to take a husband,'"

### 33.
"'He who desires [to marry] quickly takes his jungkat, the one he has long intended. Once the jungkat is taken, it is presented to the parents of the young man who wishes to marry. After receiving the jungkat, the elders conclude the arrangement.'"

### 34.
"'When the discussions of both parties are settled, they are brought together at the home of the bride. After the bride and groom meet, the priest arrives, carrying a copper bowl engraved on the outside with animal figures, filled with holy water.'"

### 35.
"'The bride and groom come forward; when they are near, the priest draws water and sprinkles it on their heads—both bride and groom—while reciting prayers to their gods, asking for the blessing and well-being of Lord Brahma, the beloved.'"

### 63.
## Kinanthi  1.

### 63.
"In this place there is no shaman; if someone falls ill, or wishes to have a child, it is the priest who attends them, carrying a copper bowl already filled with holy water."

### 2.
"He continually prays, asking for healing from sickness; for those who desire a child, their prayer goes to the gods, and for the welfare of the parents, the means is simply to give them drink."

### 3.
"He is anointed all over with holy water from the mountains; by the power of the Great Lord, the sick recover, and those who wish for a child are granted their wish—there is no anxiety in the heart."

### 4.
"When someone dies, they are cleansed with holy water, dressed in fine garments, and given equal honours; the corpse is treated like a bride and groom being carried to Mount Brahma."

### 5.
"When they reach the edge, the body is quickly cast into the crater, accompanied by all the prayers—that is the custom now." The young lord bowed and spoke of the bowl filled with holy water.

### 6.
"What of the ancient customs concerning the birth of a newborn child—the severing of the navel cord, the offerings of many kinds, and what is permissible according to religious law?" The sage spoke softly.

### 7.
"The story from ancient times: the command of the Supreme God was delivered by the god Narada to King Kanwa, to establish the Buddhist religion and all its divisions."

### 8.
"He was ordered to appoint priests and to establish markers, so that there be no confusion in their divisions nor disturbance in the practice of religion." Thus spoke the god Narada: "Hail, Kanwa, O king!"

### 9.
"I am entrusted with these words by the Supreme Lord; you are commanded to regulate the practices of religion. This is the king's decree: religion shall be the guide."

### 10.
"Its authority is the true essence, prevailing over the world; the ruler who is empowered commands his servants, thus it becomes the adornment of the realm and the glory of the kingdom."

### 11.
"Blessed are the people who submit and are steadfast in religion; the practices of the faith must be carried out according to its ordinances. What has already been revealed, let none contradict."

### 12.
"First is its priest; second, its markers; third, its form of worship; fourth, its conduct; fifth, its ascetic practices; sixth, its festivals."

### 13.
"Seventh are its prohibitions; eighth, its permissions; ninth, its penalties; tenth, when someone dies; eleventh, the disposal of the dead; twelfth, its rites of honour."

### 14.
"As it is written in the sacred texts, the six religions are distinguished—whichever one desires, let none be careless, but penetrate deeply and be steadfast in God."

### 15.
"The first is the religion of Sambu; its priest is called Pramana; its followers are called Sasewasogata; they use an emblem called a walantaga."

### 16.
"The jewel-shaped emblem is the culmination of their worship; they use it as a symbol in their homage to the images, following the way of imitation. In the month of Kartika, this is not permitted."

### 17.
"They eat only tubers during the month of Palguna; during the festival they may eat nothing salty. Their prohibition: one may not marry a relative of grandmother's lineage."

### 18.
"Aunts, uncles, siblings, grandchildren, and nieces/nephews from the male line are prohibited; but from the female line it is permitted. Liver meat is not eaten, but it is allowed as medicine."

### 19.
"Destroying a great stone shrine is forbidden; using iron is not allowed, but it is permitted as a tool. When a married person dies, the widow may perform self-sacrifice or be sacrificed, if it is the husband who dies."

### 20.
"If a woman does not sacrifice herself when her husband dies, she may have her head shaved; if a man dies and his wife does not follow him in death, he may be circumcised posthumously. If a child is born female, she may be circumcised."

### 21.
"In wedding customs, they use a special platform; the groom steps over a spinning wheel, a spindle, a winnowing basket, a rice-steamer, a cooking ladle, and a charcoal iron; the bride steps over a crowbar."

### 22.
"A plough, a hoe, and a mattock—when the appointed time comes, the corpse is prepared; that is, it is cared for, placed in a worthy location, and tended with great care."

### 23.
"The funeral offerings for the dead include glutinous rice cakes and white sticky rice; if one wishes to honour them, all debts must be paid. The religion of Sambu has been explained. Second is the religion of Brahma, and its priest of the faith."

### 24.
"It is called the Brahmana religion; its followers are called Sasewasogata; they use an emblem—a single copper trident; the trident is ornamented and worn on the chest."

### 25.
"They pay homage to fire and to the Lord of Fire; their way is steadfastness of heart. In the month of Sitra, they may not eat leafy vegetables; in the month of Srawana, it is also forbidden."

### 26.
"They face upward; during the festival they eat only sweet things. Their prohibition: one may not marry a relative of grandmother's lineage—aunts and uncles from the male line of descent."

### 27.
"From the female line it is permitted; they may not kill fire; they do not eat beef; they may not pick seeds that are just sprouting; and they may not use tin."

### 28.
"As for their permissions: they may marry a relative of grandmother's lineage—aunts and uncles from the female line—and may marry siblings' children, nieces/nephews, grandchildren from that line."

### 29.
"From both the female and male lines, marrying a niece or a teacher's child is permitted; they may eat beef if it is used as medicine; and they are allowed to use tin."

### 30.
"If used as a tool, women may be shaved; once the monthly period is finished, and for widow sacrifice following the dead, only-children, both male and female, may be circumcised or have a tooth-filing."

### 31.
"The penalty for marriage: before the bride and groom meet, they must use a ritual curtain; the family of the bride and the family of the groom must engage in merrymaking."

### 32.
"There is dancing and drinking; when someone dies, the corpse is cremated. The cleansing of the body is done with water and lime leaves; if one wishes to honour them, offerings of rice are made. Such are the customs I follow here."

### 33.
"The religion of Endra is the third; its priest is called Sakrana; its followers are Sasewasogata; they use an emblem—a single thunderbolt made of wood."

### 34.
"A thunderbolt-shaped piece of wood is worn as a hair ornament; they pay homage to the mountain and to the moon. Their way is one of contentment and acceptance; the month of Naya is their time for asceticism."

### 35.
"They may not carry a child during the month of Asuji; they may not engage in intercourse. During the festival they eat only bitter things. Their prohibition: one may not marry a relative of grandmother's lineage."

### 36.
"Aunts, uncles from the ancestors, male and female, and marrying siblings' children from the male or female line: one may not catch fish that are spawning."

### 37.
"Eating eggs is not allowed; throwing rubbish on the ground is forbidden; killing elephants is not permitted; using copper is prohibited unless used as a tool. Permission: one may marry..."

### 38.
"Those who are siblings' children from the female line may marry nieces/nephews and grandchildren from the male line or female line; eggs are permitted if used as medicine."

### 39.
"As medicine it is allowed; women may be shaved; men and women, youths and maidens—if one of them dies, the other may perform sacrificial death; a woman, if she wishes to remarry..."

### 40.
"Circumcision is permitted for males at birth; the child of a shaman may be exempted from the prohibitions. When performing marriage rites, they use a ritual canopy and a bridal dais."

### 41.
"The family of the groom and the family of the bride engage in symbolic verbal combat as a sign of refusal. When someone dies, the corpse is buried or interred in the earth."

### 42.
"In caves or deep ravines the body is laid to rest, cleansed with coconut oil and lime. For the honouring rites, various offerings are made. The durma metre now describes this in its fourth song."

## Durma 1.

### 1.
"Now the religion of Wisnu is described: its religious leader is called Ramana, its followers are Sasewasogata. They use as a certain emblem a single chakra, made of iron."

### 2.
"The iron is fashioned into a chakra and carried; they pierce the iron [for offerings]. Old palm sugar is their oblation to water and to falling rain. Their way is to follow the body's urges; their ascetic practice is during the month of Manggasri."

### 3.
"They may not eat pork; in the month of Jita they may not eat mutton. During the festival, what tastes savory and rich is prohibited. They may not marry [certain relatives]."

### 4.
"Grandmother's sisters, aunts, and uncles from the male lineage: they may not marry nieces/nephews. They may not kill creatures that are living, nor may they [harm] them."

### 5.
"Killing a garuda, boar, horse, turtle, softshell turtle, or crocodile is forbidden. Drinking and eating what intoxicates is not allowed. Using bronze is prohibited. Their permission: to marry a grandmother's relative."

### 6.
"Aunts and uncles from the female lineage, they may marry such relatives. Siblings, grandchildren, and nieces/nephews from the female line: they may marry a teacher's child."

### 7.
"Intoxicants are permitted if used as medicine; bronze may be used as a tool. A woman who is ill may have her head shaved. For a man, if he dies in battle [his body may be treated accordingly]."

### 8.
"A woman may perform sacrificial death, if not dead in battle. But asceticism together [as a couple] is not allowed. A woman may be circumcised; a man may have his tooth filed if he is the child of a shaman."

### 9.
"The penalty for marriage: they use ceremonial patah [ornaments] and kembar mayang [bridal decorations], with offerings in a mound. The parents of the bride and groom eat one banana together, half each. When someone dies, [the body] is cast away."

### 10.
"'To the river they cast the corpse, sprinkling it with water and flowers in abundance. If they wish to honour it, the means is offerings asking for grace. The fifth religion: Bayu is its leader.'"

### 11.
"'In this religion, the priest is called Bimana. Its followers, the Sasewasogata, all use as an emblem a single leather satya [mark], fashioned as a navel, carried as a charm on the head.'"

### 12.
"'Their offerings are made to the source of the wind, to the falling rain, and to the stars. Their way is one of forgiveness; their ascetic practice is in the month of Puseki. They may not eat four-legged meat.'"

### 13.
"'During the festival, only sour things may be eaten. The prohibitions: they may not marry grandmothers, aunts, nieces/uncles, or grandchildren from the male or female lineage.'"

### 14.
"'They may not marry nieces/nephews nor a teacher's child. They may not kill animals that are suckling young. They may not eat monitor lizard, nor may they use perfume.'"

### 15.
"'Cutting nails and wearing silver are forbidden; killing geckos, house lizards, and skinks is not permitted. Their permission: they may marry siblings from the male or female lineage.'"

### 16.
"'They may eat monitor lizard if used as medicine; they may use perfume or silver if used as a tool. Women may be shaved. For a new widow, the man [who marries her] may have his tooth filed.'"

### 17.
"'If one becomes the religious leader, the penalty for marriage: the husband must use a betel-box as his symbol. When the groom wishes to hold a procession, the bride's family processes.'"

### 18.
"'They use a wall as a shield—if it falls, the procession is certain; if it does not fall, the procession returns and must change the bride. If someone dies, the corpse is taken to the grave.'"

### 19.
"'It is placed in the open field or on the mountain, which is the proper place for laying out the dead. The funeral offerings are sprinkled with rainwater if they wish to honour them, with various means. The sixth religion is Kala.'"

### 20.
"'The religious leader is called Kalana. Its followers, the Sasewasogata, all use as an emblem a single lingga, made of ivory or bone, fashioned as a phallus.'"

### 21.
"'It is worn as a necklace for their worship—to all kinds of strange things and trees, stones, and the like. Their way is one of shyness; their ascetic practice is in the month of Manggala, when they go into retreat.'"

### 22.
"'They may not sleep in a cave in the month of Padrawana. They are forbidden to eat salt; during the festival, only spicy things. Their prohibitions: they may not marry [certain relatives].'"

### 23.
"'Nieces/nephews, older relatives, a teacher's child, or those of a different caste—such as the Brahma caste—they may not marry. The Waisya caste and all its kind [are also prohibited].'"

### 24.
"'They may not break open the earth that contains the goddess [of fertility], nor the earth that is like a bull's hump. They may not kill dogs, centipedes, or scorpions.'"

### 25.
"'They may not eat coconut; wearing gold is forbidden. Their permission: they may marry a relative; the young may marry within their own caste; eating coconut is permitted [as medicine].'"

### 26.
"'If used as medicine, it is permitted; wearing gold if used as a tool is also permitted. The penalty for marriage: they use a flat [ceremonial object]; the bride's family [participates].'"

### 27.
"'Wrestling with the groom's family—whoever is defeated may be ransomed. And during the procession, they use intoxicating drink; whoever becomes intoxicated, the corpse is treated accordingly.'"

### 28.
"'It is given to wild animals, the funeral arrangement of the body. It is sprinkled with water mixed with widara leaves. If they wish to honour [the dead], the means is by... Whoever does not follow the religion'"

### 29.
"'If one violates a single [rule], a penalty applies. For the Sambu religion, the punishment is to be washed away; for the Brahma religion, the punishment is to be burned; for the Endra religion, the punishment is to be buried alive.'"

### 30.
"'For the Wisnu religion, the punishment is to be drowned; for the Bayu religion, the punishment is to be exposed; for the Kala religion, the punishment is to be impaled. My child, this concludes the Buddhist religion.'"

### 31.
"'Here on Tengger, they follow the Brahma religion. The copper bowl filled with holy water is a legacy from the disciples of old. In every village, there is surely one.'"

### 32.
"'The one who keeps [the bowl] is the village elder, as told from long ago. The mountain that stands in the middle of the sea of sand is the abode of Lord Brahma.' The prince replied with deep gratitude:"

### 33.
"'You, my lord, have explained the Buddhist religion—truly it is still practiced today. But when it comes to marriage, there are some practices influenced by Javanese customs.' The sage nodded in agreement."

### 34.
"Astonished, [the sage] said: 'My child, let me ask you—the practices of your religion, the prophethood—are they the same as my religion?' The prince replied: 'There are some differences, only slight.'"

### 35.
"'The practices and what is forbidden...' The sage spoke gently: 'Prince, if you are willing, I wish to know—come, explain to me, my son.' The prince bowed low: 'Jurudemung, the true word.'"

## Jurudêmung 1.

### 1.
"In the book Bayanudayan, it describes the sharia of the six true Prophets who upheld the noble religion. This is what has been practiced by the communities, each differing from one another."

### 2.
"First is the law of the exalted Prophet Adam. His profession of faith: 'I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Adam is the father of mankind'—that is the meaning: truly I bear witness."

### 3.
"There is no other Lord but Allah, the Exalted in attributes, and I bear witness that Prophet Adam is truly the vessel of the Supreme Being. His prayer times are ten: three during the day and seven at night, completing ten times."

### 4.
"Prince Jayengsari explained the prophetic religions: Adam, Seth, Noah, Abraham, David, Moses, and Jesus, up to the sharia of the final Prophet, Muhammad the Messenger of God. Ki Buyut told the story of 'Sri-Sadana,' beginning with Dewi Sri becoming a rice pest guard for Ken Raketan, the child of Nyai Wrigu who was favored by the gods. She was rescued by Ki Wrigu, who performed the necessary rites according to Dewi Sri's instructions, until the rice pest dispersed and Dewi Sri returned to the heavens together with Dewi Tiksnawati."

### 5.
"There are ten prayer times: three during the day and seven at night, completing ten times. The scripture revealed is ten volumes, brought by the Angel Gabriel."

### 5.
"Fasting is eleven months [permitted], and one month obligatory."

### 6.
"'Fasting' means abstaining. 'White fasting' means fasting on the 14th, 15th, and 16th [of the lunar month]."

### 7.
"'Black fasting' means on the 21st and 30th, once completed, the breaking of the fast is at sunset. What is permitted to eat is meat."

### 8.
"Animals that are beneficial, the taking of their lives [is regulated]. Marrying a sibling of the same birth—on the same day of weaning—is allowed if they are united. A sibling not born from the same womb [may also be married]."

### 9.
"But after Prophet Adam died, his successor was his son, named Prophet Seth. It is decreed that if one marries one's own sibling, it is permitted."

### 10.
"Relatives and cousins and their descendants—if they marry without a formal wedding contract, as long as both are willing, it is permitted by the elders, once they have consummated the marriage."

### 11.
"If the husband dies, the widow may not remarry during the waiting period. If the wife dies, the widower may not remarry. When a community member dies, the body is buried in the mountains."

### 12.
"Second is the sharia of the exalted Prophet Noah. His profession of faith: 'I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Noah is the caliph of Allah'—that is its meaning: truly I bear witness."

### 13.
"Truly there is no Lord but Allah, and I bear witness that Prophet Noah is truly the vicegerent of Allah. Twelve times of prayer each day and night."

### 14.
"His revealed scripture consists of sixty volumes, brought by the Angel Gabriel fifty times. Fasting is once every month, on the last day of the month, only one day and one night."

### 15.
"What is permitted as food: animals that truly eat, and insects of all kinds. But even if they truly eat, if they are insects and one does not yet desire [to eat them], or has never eaten them, they are still permitted."

### 16.
"Like a puppy that is still playing and such—if one takes it for marriage, there must be a gift of clothing from both sides, permitted by the elders of both families."

### 17.
"This constitutes the marriage [agreement]: the groom's attire is looked after by the bride, and the bride's attire is looked after by the groom. If there is a divorce, the clothing and adornments are returned."

### 18.
"But both are not permitted if they commit adultery. If separation [occurs] by death, both may remarry—widow and widower. When community members die, the body is immersed in water, as deep as possible."

### 19.
"Third is the sharia of the exalted Prophet Abraham. His profession of faith: 'I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Abraham is the beloved of Allah'—the meaning: I bear witness."

### 20.
"Abraham, the beloved of Allah—truly there is no Lord but Allah, and I bear witness that the Exalted One, Prophet Abraham, peace be upon him, is the friend of Allah. Prayer is each day and night, twenty-two times. His revealed scripture is ten volumes, brought sixty times by the Angel Gabriel alone."

### 21.
"Fasting every Friday, breaking the fast after sunset. What is permitted as food: meat from the proper season and fruits from trees with roots; glutinous rice may not be eaten, nor any forbidden foods."

### 22.
"What causes itching is forbidden. What is permitted: animals not in their season, meat, and drinking what is lawful and such. The one who gives in marriage is the father or the grandfather."

### 23.
"Also a male sibling—a man may practice polygamy, with two or more wives. A widow [remarrying] is not forbidden; a widower may also remarry. When community members die, the body is buried."

### 24.
"In noble earth, which is holy—that is the earth that has not been defiled, becoming polluted ground, washed by rain. A person who is wrongfully killed, whose blood is spilled [is buried there]."

### 25.
"Fourth is the sharia of Prophet Moses. His profession of faith is described: 'I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Moses is the word of Allah.'"

### 26.
"'I bear witness that there is truly no Lord but Allah the Most True, and I bear witness that Prophet Moses is the speech of Allah.' Prayer each day and night."

### 27.
"Forty times [daily prayer]; his revealed scripture is the Torah alone. When Prophet David came, his scripture changed to the Zabur alone."

### 28.
"Applying punishment according to what is right: killing the murderer, and explaining the rights of all kings to give in marriage a woman, up to a hundred wives."

### 29.
"Fasting within a year: forty days continuously, breaking the fast each evening. And every Friday fasting is permitted but not required. Eating meat is only permitted [from certain animals]."

### 30.
"Animals that chew the cud—for example, female animals that are pregnant may not be eaten. They are all permitted [otherwise]. Animals that do not chew the cud, even if pregnant or sick, [are also permitted]."

### 31.
"Marriage: it is contracted by a king or by the high authority. A widow or widower may marry. When someone dies, the body is buried in clean, fertile earth."

### 32.
"Fifth is the sharia of the exalted Prophet Jesus. His profession of faith: 'I bear witness that there is no god but Allah, and I bear witness that Jesus is the spirit of Allah'—that is its meaning: the one who truly bears witness."

### 33.
"There is no other Lord but One, Allah. And I bear witness that truly Prophet Jesus is the son of the Supreme Being. Prayer is every Sunday, only one week's duration."

### 34.
"Sixty rak'ahs; his revealed scripture is only one, the Gospel, brought by the Angel Gabriel, messenger of the Supreme Being, only twelve times. His fasting is [as follows]:"

### 35.
"Every Lenten season, they observe Pentecost. Lent begins from the death of Prophet Jesus until his resurrection—which occurred after three days—breaking the fast each night after sunset."

### 36.
"Pentecost is the time of the descent of the Holy Spirit upon the disciples of our Lord Prophet Jesus. Two days of fasting, the same Lenten breaking of the fast, then the feast of Pentecost."

### 37.
"What is permitted: every Friday they may not eat meat of animals. But they are permitted to eat fish and such; all is allowed as long as it does not cause harm."

### 38.
"Marriage: it is contracted by a king or the authorities. When the marriage contract is completed, they are given a written marriage certificate."

### 39.
"Man and woman—woman is made superior, thus she is not to be taken in polygamy. Widow or widower from divorce, while living, may not [remarry]—the two marriages [are prohibited], unless by agreement."

### 40.
"When community members die, the body is buried in earth that is not fertile, clean and level. It is most excellent if buried in a mosque, shining like raised gold."

### 66.
## Maskumambang  1.

### 66.
"The sixth and final divine law is that of the honored Prophet, the supreme messenger of the realm, Muhammad the Messenger of God."

### 2.
"Peace and blessings be upon him; after Muhammad there is no other prophet, from then until the present day."

### 3.
"Those who have adopted the holy faith throughout the land of Java, following the chosen Prophet—this is their profession of faith."

### 4.
"I bear witness that there is no god but God, and I bear witness that Muhammad is the Messenger of God"—thus is its meaning described.

### 5.
"The messenger of Allah, the Most Holy—each day his prayer, day and night, five times without fail."

### 6.

### 7.
"The first is the time of Subuh, rising at dawn, before the sun appears; it consists of only two rak'ahs."

### 8.
"The second is the time of Luhur, when the sun is at its zenith, before it begins to decline; four rak'ahs, then the greeting of peace."

### 9.
"The third is called the time of Asar, when the sun is near setting behind the mountains; four rak'ahs, then the greeting of peace."

### 10.
"The fourth is called the time of Maghrib, when the sun has set and only its red glow remains; it consists of only three rak'ahs."

### 11.
"The fifth is the time of Isya, when the sun's light has faded and is no longer visible; four rak'ahs, then the greeting of peace. After Isya, though it is sunnah, it is obligatory—the prayer called the sunnah of Witr, consisting of only two rak'ahs."

### 13.
"After the greeting, another sunnah prayer is offered, called the Talwitr rak'ah—a single rak'ah, then the greeting of peace."

### 14.
"Alms are given once every year with sincerity; those who are eligible pay the tithe; alms for the poor are given every festival day."

### 15.
"Fasting is observed each month of Ramadan, completing a full month; the fast is broken after the Maghrib prayer each night of the fast."

### 16.
"At midnight one may eat as one wishes; the pre-dawn meal is permitted, to strengthen the body so that one may steadfastly observe the religion."

### 17.
"If one is enabled by God in the month of Dhulhijjah, one may go to Mecca to perform the pilgrimage, which enters into the pillars of Islam."

### 18.
"Thus there are five: first the profession of faith; second, prayer; third, alms; fourth, fasting."

### 19.
"Fifth, going to Mecca on pilgrimage; the contents of the Qur'an are thirty juz' and one hundred and fourteen surahs."

### 20.
"It contains three kinds of revelation: first, the Nurbuwat; second, the Kukumah; third, the Wahyu Wilayah."

### 21.
"The meaning of the Wahyu Nurbuat is that one has truly become a beloved Prophet; the Wahyu Kukumah signifies..."

### 22.
"When one has truly become a king, the Wahyu Wilayah means one becomes a Waliyullah, the jewel among those who are followed. Descended from the Angel Gabriel, twenty thousand in number, the priests who lead and administer the religion."

### 24.
"The imams are divided into four schools, each at a certain time, in the Sacred Mosque, facing the House of God."

### 25.
"Facing east toward the west is Imam Shafi'i; north to south is his direction. Imam Hanafi faces west toward the east."

### 26.
"Imam Maliki faces south to north; Imam Hambali bears witness—these four are the authorities for prayer."

### 27.
"The community may choose whichever one they prefer; but for us Javanese, Imam Shafi'i is our guide."

### 28.
"Among the things that are forbidden: the meat of animals with fangs, and all birds that have talons like eagles."

### 29.
"Also animals that live in both water and land—those that are in water, it does not matter; those on land, no matter."

### 30.
"Also that which is abominable and unclean, and that which is poisonous, or that which intoxicates—all these are forbidden."

### 31.
"All things are permitted that do not fall under the forbidden; if one marries, the marriage is contracted by the father as guardian."

### 32.
"The grandfather and male siblings are obligated to act as guardian; the three closest male relatives from the father's side are those who may act as guardian."

### 33.
"Marriage is officiated by the religious leader, witnessed by four; a widower may remarry, a widow may take a husband."

### 34.
"From Prophet Seth to the Prophet Muhammad, women are permitted to enter into marriage—with cousins once and twice removed."

### 35.
"From the male and female line, and others of the family, that which is not permitted: marrying a sibling of the same parents, or a foster-sibling."

### 36.
"All siblings' relatives are not permitted; polygamy with a sibling is not allowed—one must be separate from the other."

### 37.
"A man is permitted to take up to four wives; more than that is not allowed. A male child, once of age..."

### 38.
"At twelve years, a boy must be circumcised; a girl, when of age, is circumcised at three years. If one dies..."

### 39.
"The body is buried in the earth; for those of the Shafi'i school, the body is oriented northward; for those of the Hanafi school..."

### 40.
"The Hanafi school has the grave oriented westward; the Maliki school has the grave oriented southward."

### 41.
"The Hambali school has the grave oriented eastward. As for the perfecting of the body, it originates from the divine law of Prophet Adam."

### 42.
"Until now there are only four procedures: first, cleansing with water until pure; second, the funeral prayer is recited over it."

### 43.
"Third, it is wrapped carefully so that nothing is visible; fourth, it is buried in the earth or submerged in water."

### 44.
"Thus ends the account of the prophets who held divine law—six prophets in succession, as mentioned in the Book."

### 45.
"Ki Ajar smiled and spoke softly: 'My son, as I understand it, the practices of the Buddhist religion and the prophetic tradition...'"

### 46.
"'Only the practices differ, but their essence is the same.' The young lord bowed, and Buras addressed the sage concerning the origins of the newborn child."

### 67.
## Gambuh  1.

### 67.
"The sage spoke softly: 'The tale of the newborn child and its many ceremonies dates back to the time of Sri Maha Punggung—the ancient story at court.'"

### 2.
"'There were two children, female and male: Sadana and Dewi Sri. By decree, Sadana became a king, while Sri became a serpent corpse, both children suffering misfortune.'"

### 3.
"'Both wandered aimlessly, not knowing where they were headed; the serpent corpse traveled until it reached the region of Wiratha, coming to rest in the rice fields.'"

### 4.
"'It lay coiled in the middle of the rice paddies in the village of Wasutira. The headman, a hunter named Wrigu, and his wife Nikèn Sangki, at that time she was well along in pregnancy.'"

### 5.
"'Ahead of the hunter, Kyai Wrigu served a sage named Sang Wisama, who was moved with pity and wished to give him a child; he was given instruction and charged with earnest advice.'"

### 6.
"'Hark, Wrigu, know this: you shall be granted a child. If you follow my instructions, it will bring you true fortune. As the means, you must seek and obtain "we yoga" water without delay.'"

### 7.
"'That which is called "we yoga" water has four colours—one colour from water originating from the earth, two colours from water from the sky, three colours from water originating from plants that grow.'"

### 8.
"'The four colours of water, from those which possess the essence of life—mix them for the ploughing ritual. Then drink it with your wife; if it is well-received, it will be effective.'"

### 9.
"'When she is truly pregnant, both of you come to me quickly; I wish to reveal to you the destiny of your child.' Kyai Wrigu withdrew slowly."

### 10.
"After Kèn Sangki became pregnant, both of them hurried to appear before the yogi, who spoke softly: 'Hark, Wrigu, know this—your wife is with child.'"

### 11.
"'That which is in the womb is the incarnation of a heavenly nymph—it is Dewi Tiksnawati, the Jewel of Radiance from ancient times; you have encountered true fortune.'"

### 12.
"'Dewi Sri's existence and Tiksnawati, that heavenly nymph—Sri's presence is like an essence that dwells in the nursery, and that is the path of their meeting.'"

### 13.
"'Sri and Tiksnawati are united—seek the noble serpent corpse that wears earrings of ripened rice; when you have found it, guard it with great care.'"

### 14.
"'Do not let it come to death, for if it dies, your child will perish.' Having received the sage's final words, Ki Wrigu was permitted to go home. He searched long, and it was not long before he found it."

### 15.
"On the first day of the month of Padrawana, Kyai Wrigu, exhausted, stopped to rest on a slope; he received a revelation from the great god: the place where the serpent corpse dwelled."

### 16.
"'If it allows itself to be held, spread out a fine cloth; wipe it with a white sarong scented with fragrance; sprinkle it with sweet perfume; when it is in the container there...'"

### 17.
"'Roll it up carefully and place it inside your house, in a clean place.' Thus spoke the revelation. Wrigu awoke, then went home to carry out the instructions."

### 18.
"When he arrived at the field, he quickly spread the cloth; the serpent, catching the sweet scent, slithered toward it and coiled upon the garment. Ki Wrigu bowed slowly."

### 19.
"The serpent was rolled up and brought home, placed in the nursery of Ki Wrigu and Nikèn Sangke. Their hearts were filled with wonder, for its appearance was radiant."

### 20.
"Offerings were prepared—a heap of green frogs piled high. It is told that that night the pregnancy came to term and a beautiful girl was born, filling both their hearts with joy."

### 21.
"Ki Wrigu, weary, fell asleep; the serpent appeared to him in a dream: 'Do not offer me green frogs. Offer me beautiful betel, flowers, and incense—do not let them be empty.'"

### 22.
"'This will bring about true fortune and my blessing. Give your child the name Rara Rakêtan—it is fitting. Within seven days from now...'"

### 23.
"'At night, do not sleep; during the day, you may sleep. This is for protection, so that it may come to pass, free from interference and evil schemes.' When dawn came, he awoke and carried out the instruction."

### 24.
"After his dream that night, before long people came flocking to visit the baby, bringing various gifts. Ki Wrigu and his wife gave thanks to the All-Seeing God."

### 25.
"At that time, heaven was greatly disturbed because Dewi Tiksnawati had been incarnated as a human without divine permission. Hyang Guru quickly sent a messenger."

### 26.
"Sang Hyang Kala was commanded to descend and wreak havoc; he possessed a jackal while Wrigu was asleep. The serpent appeared to Wrigu in a dream: 'Know the meaning of this—the sun is setting now.'"

### 27.
"'Sang Hyang Kala has descended to bring calamity upon your child, in the form of a jackal. Quickly ward it off! Burn brimstone at all your doorways at once.'"

### 28.
"'Inside your house, light a torch of coconut leaves three times; all through the night make the offerings: a chicken heart, a liver—prepare them for the child's portion.'"

### 29.
"'Add a winnowing basket, a broom, a dustpan, and betel-leaf rolls—do not omit them. Place an oil lamp that must not go out; circle your house at sunset, slowly.'"

### 30.
"'Recite this mantra: "O Kala, you named Wayèh," through the night your child, grandmother, will surely find safety.' Wrigu awoke and told his wife of the dream."

### 31.
"Having told his wife, they prepared everything, leaving nothing out. The jackal was bewildered, unable to enter, until dawn broke; defeated, it quickly vanished."

### 32.
"The Lord of the Universe was angry; he sent Hyang Brahma to descend, taking the form of the Lembu Gumarang bull. Ki Wrigu was sleeping; the serpent spoke to him softly in a dream."

### 33.
"'Hark, Wrigu, know this: tonight when people are asleep, there will come Hyang Brahma in the form of a bull, seeking to bring calamity upon your child. Your protective measures must be prepared.'"

### 34.
"'At the right and left of the door, place pineapple leaves that are woven; burnt coconut fibre and white string, with onion skin as incense, and as before, a torch of dried coconut palm.'"

### 35.
"'For the child's portion, offer red rice with side dishes, steamed young rice and mature rice, betel leaf and areca nut—do not omit these. Do not be afraid, outwardly or inwardly.'"

### 1.
"When circling the house, recite this mantra: 'Take care—Hong Brahmana, rasioyeh maswaena.' Truly you will find well-being." Wrigu awoke from sleep and told his wife Ken Sangki. The offerings were quickly prepared.

### 2.
"Wrigu followed the dream's instructions. When the Lembu Gumarang bull came, trying to enter the house, it was confounded by the defenses. It tried with all its might but could not prevail. Finally at dawn it vanished. [The bull] reported its failure to Hyang Guru, who quickly sent another messenger."

### 3.
"Lord Wisnu then descended, taking the form of a boar. Wrigu was sleeping during the day. The serpent-corpse gave a sign: 'Wrigu, at midnight Lord Wisnu will descend as a boar, seeking to harm your child.'"

### 4.
"'Strengthen your defenses: add single widara leaves, and the pineapple leaves from before. Change the incense burners. Burn tanjung leaves as torches. As before, present offerings of fish and red rice at your booth.'"

### 5.
"'Add betel-leaf rolls as before, and fragrant flowers. When circling the house, carry a torch and recite the mantra: "Omma suya, namartagda namandaha." Truly your child will be safe.' Wrigu awoke from sleep."

### 6.
"He quickly told his wife Ken Sangki. Everything was carried out. When the time came, the boar arrived and tried to enter the house but was overcome by the defenses. At dawn it vanished, reporting its failure."

### 7.
"Lord Pramesthi was angry and decided to descend himself. He took the form of a bird. Many of the gods followed, each taking a form as they pleased. Ki Wrigu was sleeping during the day; the serpent-corpse gave a sign."

### 8.
"'Wrigu, awake now—Lord Maheswara descends, taking the form of a bird, accompanied by the gods. They all take forms: Narada as a frog-jumper, Brahma as the Kala Gumarang, Surya as a grasshopper.'"

### 9.
"Mahadewa takes the form of a horned goat; Yama as a deer with antlers; Kuvera as a mouse; Siwah as a deer named Karandhi; Pritanjala as a small bird."

### 10.
"Rodra as a plow bull; Wisnu as a thick-boar; Bayu as a buffalo; Basuki as a male serpent; Kala as a howling dog; Chandra as a cat; all seek to harm your child."

### 11.
"'As before, fortify your house by circling it with white thread. Gather the neighbors and your friends and servants—invite them all to keep watch. There is another requirement: spread mats on the sleeping platform.'"

### 12.
"'On senthe leaves, draw pictures of a boar and a crocodile, and draw a gandhik. Make them resemble humans. Place them on the senthe leaves at sunset. Your child should be held, awake or asleep, until dawn.'"

### 13.
"'Fence the house with semburana [herbs], dalingo, shallots, and garlic. While applying these, recite the mantra: "Hong Hyang Siwah aboja, abuyana kita marta, swana maswana." It is finished—the child will surely find prosperity, and all in the house.'"

### 14.
"'And further: tonight, do not kill anything you see. Do not dare to strike, because Hyang Guru will descend. Kala Kuthana will come first—he is the king of poison and sorcery.'"

### 15.
"'From Yomani hell they come, taking the form of corpse-spells, in all kinds of shapes, appearing like mosquitoes and such. Do not let yourself kill them, or truly your child will fall victim to their schemes.'"

### 16.
"'The requirement: honor them three times each night, calling on Kala Kuthana at the time when the king of corpses appears. There are three corpses, from sunset—this is how you honor them:'"

### 17.
"'Ahangko Sang Nuriswa ta, kang kesdya ayu aweka, hya ngayu muli angkosa, karengketan tata nama, walungyanto ya wanggawa'—the first one comes at sunset. This is how you honor him."

### 18.
"'Ahangko Sang Neris tekang, kisdya ayu Akwiki Hyang, ngayu muli hangko saka, reng tekanta tan na mawa, lunganta ywa gawa purna'—the second one comes at midnight. This is how you honor him."

### 19.
"'Ahangko Sang Naris tekang, ke sedya ayu Akwiki, ywangayu muli angkosa, saka reng tekan tan nana, mawa lungyanta ywang gawa'—Wrigu, remember well, honor them three times each night."

### 20.
"Their names, each one: the first is Sang Nuriswa, the second is Sang Niris, the third is Sang Naris. They are all the same in nature. It is Sang Kala Kuthana who rules the corpse-spells. Each time you give honor:"

### 21.
"'Speak sharply as if scolding. Then all the corpse-spells will flee, none daring to approach. Your child will find well-being.' Wrigu awoke from sleep. By the will of the gods, he was given remembrance without forgetfulness."

### 22.
"He told his wife; all the offerings were prepared. It is told that Hyang Guru and all the gods descended but were all defeated, unable to carry out their schemes. Finally they all vanished, and the baby remained safe."

### 23.
"'There was no disturbance at all. That, Buras, is the origin—the tradition continues to this day.' Buras gaped, speechless, nodding with his mouth open. The prince snapped his fingers; Buras startled, scrambled, and bowed repeatedly, remaining wordless."

### 24.
"The prince asked the sage: 'How was Dewi Sri released from her state as a serpent-corpse?' The sage replied: 'Lord Jagadnata saw that the schemes against Sri were thwarted.'"

### 25.
"He quickly sent heavenly nymphs to summon Dewi Sri, wishing to make her complete among the nymphs. They descended and met Sri, delivering the command. Sri bowed her head in homage but made a condition."

### 26.
"'Only when I am released from this suffering, together with my younger brother Sadana, will I become a goddess in time.' The nymph messenger returned. Now Prince Sadana had already been restored by Sri Wiku Ngatasmaruta, who gave him his daughter [in marriage]."

### 27.
"Named Dewi Laksmitawahni—but it was the will of Hyang Giri that in time she would bear children, to continue the lineage. The serpent-corpse, hearing the words of the heavenly nymphs and learning of her younger brother's fate, was deeply moved."

### 28.
"She writhed, clearly asking for release. She was immediately released and restored—the serpent form vanished and Sri was revealed. Then she was to be taken, but Sri still refused, telling the heavenly nymphs what troubled her heart."

### 29.
"'To abandon Wrigu's child—if she should fall victim to the gods' schemes and die, that would be like my own parent's doing. How great would be my heart's sorrow! As long as she lives, if she feels cared for, I am obliged to care for her in return.'"

### 30.
"'My meaning is this: I feel that I have been cared for, so my repayment is to care for her.' All the heavenly nymphs felt that Sri's words were proper. There was one nymph named Dewi Nariti who whispered to Dewi Sri:"

### 31.
"'If Ki Wrigu's child is the incarnation of Tiksnawati, then the schemes against her are great, because she incarnated without permission. Hence Hyang Giri's anger.' Dewi Sri replied: 'Lord Jagadnata truly has the quality of love and mercy, and is all-powerful.'"

### 32.
"'But He does not will to exercise His power over the sinless. What then of ourselves? Would mercy be shown to us without wrongdoing? What is the way of those who are hoped for?'"

### 33.
"'If He were to investigate the actions of the almighty and even seek to exert His power—if Hyang Giri were to do that, what nature would that be? It would truly be confusion—surely all His creations [would suffer].'"

### 34.
"The heavenly nymphs, hearing the noble words, at once vanished. Ki Wrigu and his wife wished to cleanse the serpent's place. When they drew back the screen, they were astonished—the serpent was gone, and in its place was a woman."

### 35.
"Exceedingly beautiful was her appearance. Wrigu and his wife both spoke: Dewi Sri then told everything from beginning to end. Wrigu offered many words, wishing to prepare refreshments. Sri did not wish for anything—she only asked for."

### 36.
"Beautiful betel and flowers, beautiful bananas and incense. Wrigu withdrew, saying he would obey. Then the heavenly nymphs descended to meet Sri, delivering the command of Hyang Giri, making known the words that came forth."

### 69.
## Mijil  1.

### 69.
"Hyang Daruna and Dewi Daruni, his sister, committed a shameful act in heaven, engaging in improper conduct—that is the Lambangsari union with one's sibling."

### 2.
"Both were now cast out, descending to the mortal world. Hyang Daruna was incarnated as the newborn son of Ki Subandha, while Dewi Daruni was also incarnated."

### 3.
"To Rakêtan, Ki Wrigu's child, as her manifestation—the essence of the lady Tiksnawati. When both come of age, by the will of the gods, they shall become husband and wife."

### 4.
"In time she will bear a daughter, who becomes the queen of Wiratha, descending from the line of kings of the land of Java." Dewi Sri, upon hearing this, was glad at heart.

### 5.
"She promptly said: 'I obey the command of the All-Seeing God, but I ask for a covered carriage with a driver—the Lembu Gumarang bull and the Naga Serang as whip.'"

### 6.
"After Dewi Sri had finished speaking, the nymphs quickly vanished and arrived before Hyang Giri, reporting all that Sri had said."

### 7.
"After the nymphs had departed, Wrigu and his wife offered betel and incense. Sri had finished chewing betel when Wrigu said softly: 'Rakêtan now has a fever.'"

### 8.
"Sri said: 'That is the influence of the incarnation that has taken hold. Previously it was the incarnation of Tiksnawati, now it has been replaced by the lady Daruni, for Tiksnawati is here by my side.'"

### 9.
"Kyai Wrigu and Nikèn Sangki were astonished to see that she was a twin without equal. Sri said: 'Lick the fontanelles of your child, little girl.'"

### 10.
"'And the navel string, cut into two pieces, place three small coins to the left of its base, and while licking, make a wish—truly your child will be safe.'"

### 11.
"'When your child is sleeping, heed my warning: do not kiss it before the fontanelle has closed—do not dare to pinch its cheek, Wrigu.'"

### 12.
"'Do not compare it with the dead; the consequence is bad—nothing good will come if you do that. Care for it properly. I promise you that in the future your child...'"

### 13.
"'...will help continue the line of kings in the land of Java, an extraordinary blessing.' Wrigu and Sangki gave great thanks, took their leave, and went to lick their child—she was well and beautiful."

### 14.
"Before long the nymphs arrived, bringing the covered carriage as Sri had requested. Sri and Tiksnawati mounted it, along with the heavenly maidens, and the carriage sped away."

"Section 4: Prince Jayengsari with his younger sister and servant continued their journey, arriving at Klakah village, lodging in the home of Umbul Sadyana, observing the fire of Mount Lamongan at night. They continued on to Kandhangan village in the Lumajang area, meeting Sheikh Amongbudi who taught about ablution and prayer. At Argapura they met Sheikh Wahdat who explained: 1. The Essence, Attributes, Names, and Actions of God; 2. The explanation of the Twenty Attributes."

### 14.
"Wrigu and Sangki entered the nursery—it was empty, nothing to be seen. Great was their sorrow; but by the will of the gods, their hearts were pacified, and they recited blessings over their child."

### 16.
"All this is the origin from ancient times, continuing to the present, passed down to those who observe it—the rice barn, the mortar, the pounding shed, and all the doors, every night."

### 17.
"On Fridays they make offerings of flowers and ointment, along with the pounding shed; they set up an elegant headcloth canopy, called a pasrèn, covered with a mosquito net."

### 18.
"They offer a woven bamboo tray filled with items, water in a bowl—this is the origin of farmers who wish to harvest. Before beginning the harvest, they make offerings: flowers, fragrant ointment, bananas, and beautiful betel."

### 19.
"A new mat and a white sarong, with the hope that Sri will come—this is the origin of the dishes prepared: vegetable soup, bean sprouts with chicken coconut sauce, for the birth of the newborn with its ceremonies."

### 20.
"Thus ends the tale of the goddess. As she sat close, tears streaming down, her voice trembling, she said: 'Brother, this place feels cold to me—day and night it is chilly, and I cannot find...'"

### 21.
"'...brother Jayengresmi, who is missing—they say we will meet soon.' Jayengsari, hearing his sister's words, felt a tightness in his heart; he spoke softly: 'Ah, my sibling...'"

### 22.
"'Do not rush, wait a few days—surely you will meet him.' The sage quickly approached the lady and spoke sweetly: 'Child, do not worry; in the future you will meet him.'"

### 23.
"'And your brother has already taken a wife, of exquisite beauty, and he brings gifts for you—jewelry and garments for women, sarongs of many colours, smooth and fine.'"

### 24.
"Rancangkapti's heart was eased; she spoke softly: 'Is your story really true? For now it only pleases my heart. If it's a lie, I'll pinch you!' The sage chuckled."

### 25.
"'My story is not a lie, child, but I warn you: do not be hasty or impatient, and do not return to Giri. If you go home, surely you will not be able to meet him.'"

### 26.
"'Yes, brother, I will not go home—I promise we will meet. And as for brother, let him take his time.' Jayengsari soothed his sibling; the younger sister was calmed and addressed the great sage."

### 27.
"'My lord, if it pleases you, show me the whereabouts of my elder brother Jayengresmi.' The great sage replied to the question: 'It is a grave prohibition, my child, to reveal that.'"

### 28.
"'For it is still concealed by the gods, meant to become a destiny. Both of you shall undertake a journey that will truly come to pass—a path of asceticism that is great, if you persevere to the end.'"

### 29.
"'Suffer hardship and never complain—accept with an inward steadfastness. Remember that God never sleeps, sending down rain to soften the heart, generous and loving, truly protecting.'"

### 30.
"'Complete your journey, my child, and hurry onward. Do not dwell on former glory—now you are like a withered leaf on the ocean, drifting with the waves of the sea.'"

### 31.
"The young lord, with tears, spoke softly: 'O illustrious one, your words bring clarity—steadfastness of heart, trust in God—the sage's blessing I humbly seek.'"

### 32.
"'Now, while it is still morning, allow me to take my leave, my lord, and go.' 'Young lady, you ask permission of the sage, and ask also of all the maidservants.'"

### 33.
"The lady said: 'Permit me, grandfather, to follow my brother on his journey. Your blessing is all I need, grandfather—may we be safe and soon find the one we seek.'"

### 34.
"'Yes, my son and you, my child—only my blessing: may God protect you both, may your wishes be fulfilled. Buras, I give you my blessing for a safe journey.'"

### 35.
"Buras bowed and spoke, greatly honouring the words of the illustrious one. The young lord and his sister had already departed; the great sage accompanied them to the right and left, with blessings. Outside, a carriage arrived."

### 36.
"Both were embraced and clasped close, then they set off together. The maidservants crowded behind, offering prayers for their well-being—truly great was their love for the handsome lord."

### 70.
## Asmaradana  1.

### 70.
"The two set off on their journey, Buras always behind. When the lady grew weary, she was carried on the back. They climbed the slopes of the mountains, descended into ravines, and traversed the great forest—no danger could be counted."

### 2.
"They paused in their walking; the sun was inclining westward. They stopped on the eastern slope of the mountain, in a cool spot shaded by bushes. Before them was a pleasant view, and the lady's heart was stirred at the sight of the settlements."

### 3.
"Green patches of vegetation stretched across the valleys; clear water flowed down into a lake like a pond. Its fish, copper-red, floated like offerings to those accustomed to the mountain."

### 4.
"Herons and egrets stood at the lake's edge, stretching their necks as if welcoming. Dragonflies darted about, crisscrossing, wanting to be seen—a soothing sight for the heart, bringing joy to the soul."

### 5.
"Prawns floated at the edge, snatched by the darting dragonflies. An egret quickly dove in and caught a fish, then flew away. At the same time, the dragonflies arrived above the beautiful lady—a fish and a prawn."

### 6.
"They were placed before the lady, who was picking flowers. She was startled to see the fish fall, together with the prawn. She said quickly: 'Buras, here is some fallen fish, together with a prawn!'"

### 7.
"'Quick, catch them both, they fell from the sky! Brother, look what I've got—a fish as big as a coconut-shell, and a prawn about a hand-span long, falling right in front of me!'"

### 8.
"'If only there were a kitchen, I'd cook them in a pot!' The young lord said softly: 'If you'd like them fried, Buras, go find some kamal fruit and coarse salt to season the fish.'"

### 9.
"Buras went off and returned, bringing kamal fruit and salt. The lady said softly: 'Grind them on a stone, add some water, and find some blêkêtupuk leaves to wrap the fish for cooking.'"

### 10.
"The fish was gutted and cleaned. Buras then quickly built a fire and covered it; the wrapped packets were buried in the embers. When cooked, he took them out and presented them to the beautiful lady, who opened them before her elder brother."

### 11.
"'Brother, these blêtupuk leaves—mother used to say that when used for wrapping fish, the taste is no different from any other wrapped fish. Buras, try some.'"

### 12.
"He crouched and ate, even the burnt bits he consumed. The lady asked softly: 'Well, Buras, is it true—does it taste like fish?' Buras smacked his lips and nodded. Rancangkapti was greatly pleased."

### 13.
"After both had eaten, the prawn and fish were not finished; Ki Buras quickly cleared them away. As the sun was about to set, they continued their journey, arriving at a field at dusk, far from any settlement."

### 14.
"'What do you wish, little sister? Should we sleep in a settlement? Since it is already late, let us climb up into the watch-hut in the middle of the rice field—tonight if we sleep, we can stay there.'"

### 15.
"'Tomorrow at daybreak we'll continue.' The three climbed up; it was the full moon, bright and shining. Many things could be seen—wild boar with their young passed by. The lady asked her brother..."

### 16.
"'Brother, a mouse from Mount Kawi—big, tall, with cloven hooves, how wide its snout is!' 'Little sister, that is called a wild boar.' Then there appeared a striped tiger about to pounce on the young of the boar."

### 17.
"'The three of us are far away.' The lady said to her brother: 'Is that a cat? It's so big, with a sour-face, all striped! Buras, catch it quickly—I want to keep it as a pet!' Ki Buras just stood still."

### 18.
"The tiger was no longer visible. Buras exclaimed, 'God be praised! That, my lady, is called a sima—a tiger often eats people! If I had gone to catch it, my buttocks would be trembling, soaked in sweat, and I'd be too terrified to move!'"

### 19.
"Rancangkapti spoke softly: 'Here I do not intend to harm; there, it is the same. So, Buras, be trusted—your steady heart would be useless if you met danger.' Buras replied, 'Indeed.'"

### 20.
"At midnight the lady slept; Buras dozed, nodding off. The young lord alone remained awake, praying to God for the well-being of his father and his elder brother who wandered far, and for his own prayers."

### 21.
"'May safety be upon you and your younger sister; may your journey have no difficulties; may you achieve your desires; may you in the future meet with the one you seek.' At the time when the third drum sounded..."

### 22.
"The birds' songs filled the air, those that call at dawn—the cing-cinggoling, the thilang pênthèt, the cocak, the kèkèt, the sikatan, the srigunting, the dragonflies, the starling, the turtledove—all calling from the sky."

### 23.
"The lady awoke from her sleep: 'Brother, what time is it? The birds are already singing, and I hear the voices of people pounding rice—a hum of activity. This is the time to rise, brother—let us go to the spring.'"

### 24.
"'Buras, are you sleeping? You're crouching but snoring, dribbling, your lap is wet! Hey, Buras, wake up—it's already dawn!' He startled and said, 'My lady, I was not asleep!'"

### 25.
"Both laughed heartily: 'Yes, you were not asleep—just dozing a little!' The lady and the young lord climbed down from the watch-hut, Ki Buras always behind. They reached the spring and bathed."

### 26.
"Wrapped in a thin cloth, the two continued on. Their journey is not described. At the time of Asar they arrived at Klakah, at the foot of Mount Lamongan, where the beauty of the village was visible, spread out with abundant wealth."

### 27.
"And those who were working in the fields and paddies—the young lord paused in his walk, along with his younger sister, in the midst of the fields, gazing at all the villagers who were busy at work."

### 28.
"Seeing the newcomers, Ki Buras was told to ask the name of the village and its headman. The one asked replied softly: 'This is the village of Klakah, and the headman is named Ki Umbul Sadyana.'"

### 29.
"'Who is it that you accompany? His appearance is not that of a commoner. Where do you come from, and where are you headed?' Ki Buras replied: 'The one I follow is originally from Surapringga. He intends to go to Banyuwangi, but has lost his way and does not know the road. Now my lord wishes, since the journey is interrupted, to find lodging for one night; tomorrow we will continue.'"

### 31.
"The field worker, upon hearing this, set down his tools and quickly went to inform the headman. After everything was reported, Kyai Umbul Sadyana said: 'Quickly, serve them and bring them here immediately.'"

### 32.
"When he reached the field, he said respectfully: 'Young sir, you are invited.' They then entered his house; after their journey, Kyai Umbul Sadyana greeted them outside the village, and the two were already accompanied."

### 33.
"They proceeded into the pavilion and were seated on the bamboo platform. Ki Umbul did not wish to sit level with them. The young lord said to the householder: 'Grandfather, do not stay below; I would feel awkward. It is better that you come up.'"

### 34.
"Ki Buyut came up and said softly: 'Young sir, forgive my lack of manners. Along with both of you, where do you come from and what is your purpose in coming to the village of Klakah? This Argasonya region has many dangers.'"

### 35.
"'Grandfather, I am from Gresik, called Jayengsari, and my younger sister is named Rara Rancangkapti. This companion is Buras. The reason I am like a sunken pucung fruit is that I seek my elder brother.'"

### 71.
## Pocung  1.

### 71.
"Without further tale of his travels: Jayengresmi by name, wandering, arrived here, but was not found—no news was obtained at all."

### 2.
"The younger sister is deeply devoted to her brother, cannot bear to be parted; finally she was left behind, lonely, and cannot be stopped from seeking him."

### 3.
"Kyai Umbul, upon hearing this, was deeply moved; he said softly: 'Young sir, be strong of heart—your elder brother, for now, is still safe.'"

### 4.
"'The will of God is not yet favourable.' The lady said: 'Grandfather, so he is still alive—my brother Jayengresmi whom I seek.'"

### 5.
"'Thank God, if the promise does not lead to death—someday, grandfather, we shall meet.' Umbul replied: 'Yes, indeed, that is the truth.'"

### 6.
"'Allow me to offer what I have—it is only modest, as medicine for the weary.' The young lord bowed, and both of them ate."

### 7.
"When finished, the remains were cleared away by Buras. 'Boy, you may eat now—do not be shy.' After the meal, it was the time of sunset."

### 8.
"The stars began to appear, sparkling in the sky; it was the dry season, clear in the heavens. The sound of Mount Lamongan rumbled."

### 9.
"The young lord listened for a while to what was heard. Ki Umbul Sadyana said: 'At the peak of the mountain there is a crater that spews fire.'"

### 10.
"'It is best viewed from the lake behind; it is not far from the village—Tirtamaya, where the reflection of the mountain is clearly visible.'"

### 11.
"The young lady said: 'Brother, I want to come too.' Ki Umbul Sadyana and his two guests set out and arrived at the edge of the lake."

### 12.
"The great lake Tirtamaya was very deep, stretching wide; the far edge on one side was visible reaching the flanks of the mountain."

### 13.
"Standing tall like a steaming cone, the mountain's reflection floated on the lake, visible, spewing fire from its crater."

### 14.
"The sparks that emerged looked like fragrant flowers, spraying without cease, scattering in the sky, falling like a rain of fire."

### 15.
"The hearts of the young lord and the beautiful lady were stilled, unable to contain what was seen in the sky—the lovely reflection in the lake seemed charming."

### 16.
"Above and below became one—the flowers of fire; both were struck with wonder, seeing the fire emerge, incomparable, causing astonishment in their hearts."

### 17.
"At the edge of the lake they stayed through the night, none of them sleeping. When the time of dawn approached, the young lord took leave of Ki Umbul Sadyana."

### 18.
"'Forgive me, grandfather, for I must go; since it is already daylight, I shall continue from here, to carry on my journey in search of my brother.'"

### 19.
"Ki Sadyana said: 'Young sir, if it pleases you, return to the house—the old woman has prepared some breakfast, whatever the mountain provides.'"

### 20.
"'Yes, grandfather, I am most grateful for your kindness to me, but let us hurry while it is still morning.' Kyai Umbul bowed and said: 'As you wish.'"

### 21.
"'I offer my prayers for the safety of your journey—may there be no obstacles.' 'Indeed, grandfather, the same to you—may those who remain find happiness and well-being.'"

### 22.
"They departed from Mount Lamongan and continued their journey, traveling together with his sister; the three of them, with Buras behind, never straying."

### 23.
"Their journey onward is not told—they arrived at the village of Kandhangan, at the foot of the mountain, in the Semeru region, territory of the realm of Lumajang."

### 24.
"They stopped beneath a great tree at the edge of a spring; the lady cupped the water to drink, while watching the red fish swimming in great numbers."

### 25.
"Unaware of her own weariness, she was captivated watching the fish swimming about, whose place seemed to welcome the beautiful lady."

### 26.
"Before long, the headman of the village of Kandhangan arrived, named Sheikh Amongbudi; he immediately exchanged greetings with the young lord."

### 27.
"After shaking hands, Ki Sheikh said: 'Forgive my lack of manners—you, young sir, what is your name, and from where do you come?'"

### 28.
"The young lord spoke softly in reply: 'I am Jayengsekar, from the realm of Gresik—and that one there at the edge of the spring...'"

### 29.
"'...is my younger sister, named Rancangkapti. She wanders seeking our elder brother, the young lord Jayengresmi, up to the present time.'"

### 30.
"'We have not yet found him—no news at all. Uncle, what is your name?' 'Young sir, the name I go by is Amongbudi, the elder of Kandhangan.'"

### 31.
"'The reason, Sheikh of Kandhangan in Semeru: if it is agreeable, my child and your younger sister, may they be pleased to stop at my home.'"

### 32.
"'To rest the body, which is weary; later when you are refreshed, you may proceed as you wish.' The young lord replied: 'I shall follow your will.'"

### 33.
"'But I have not yet asked my younger sister—what do you wish, young lady?' Amongbudi's request: 'Yes, brother, it is good to stop at the village.'"

### 34.
"Amongbudi was exceedingly glad at heart. The young lord and his sister were already brought to his village; arriving at the house, they settled in as family."

### 35.
"His wife was told to quickly prepare offerings: betel, coffee, sweet palm sugar, and dishes of rice with various fish and meats."

### 72.
## Dhandhanggula  1.

### 1.
"The offerings were prepared and quickly set forth, arranged in rows. Sheikh Amongbudi said: 'O my child, if it pleases you, I offer this humble fare—truly it lacks good taste, for everything here is scarce, only for the sake of Allah.'"

### 2.
"'Yes, uncle, though I have only just arrived, my heart feels as if I have long been acquainted—there is no difficulty at all.' Amongbudi laughed, delighted by the prince's words. The prince and his sister ate with enjoyment. When finished, the remains were cleared away by Ki Buras, who ate until satisfied."

### 3.
"Amongbudi continued: 'Here is something to accompany the meal—coffee and sweet palm-sugar drinks.' Jayengsari was silent, his sister sitting beside him. When they had finished eating at their leisure, the prince asked gently: 'Which mountain is that which can be seen smoking?' Sheikh Amongbudi answered:"

### 4.
"'That is Semeru, the peak of the mountain, which has a crater both day and night, continuously issuing smoke. Frequently it spews forth, emitting fire, but it causes no harm. At sunset, when the sun is replaced by the moon, at the time of Maghrib, the prayer bells were struck and many santri arrived.'"

### 5.
"They took water for ablution, went to the mosque reciting blessings; the call to prayer was made, everyone fell silent. After the adhan they proceeded quickly, performing the two cycles of the Maghrib sunnah prayer. Then the iqamah was called, and they performed the obligatory Maghrib prayer. Sheikh Amongbudi served as imam; the prince as makmum, while Lady Rancangkapti followed the prayer in the women's section."

### 6.
"Along with all the female santri, until the time of Isha, after the prayer was completed, the praises and supplications were finished. The beautiful lady then returned to join her elder brother, sitting comfortably on the veranda of the prayer house. Rancangkapti asked Buras: 'Why did you not join the prayer earlier?' Buras answered with reverence:"

### 7.
"'After doing wudu I was suddenly overcome by the need to defecate. After cleaning myself, my wudu was voided. The trouble, as the story goes, was that my brother and cousin kept coming out from above. In my opinion, there is a comparison: every time I bow in prayer, the cousin pays back, not with farts but with constant gurgling—the cousin always pays out.'"

### 8.
"Mongbudi smiled and spoke gently: 'Good Buras, gurgling is nothing—it does not invalidate your wudu.' The prince said softly: 'Buras, tell the kyai—'"

ê

### 8.
"—what is the difficulty?' Buras stepped forward and said: 'O Kyai Sheikh, truly, what makes wudu valid and what makes it invalid, and likewise for prayer?'"

### 9.
